=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array points of size n and an integer m. There is another array gameScore of size n, where gameScore[i] represents the score achieved at the i^th game. Initially, gameScore[i] == 0 for all i.
You start at index -1, which is outside the array (before the first position at index 0). You can make at most m moves. In each move, you can either:

Increase the index by 1 and add points[i] to gameScore[i].
Decrease the index by 1 and add points[i] to gameScore[i].

Note that the index must always remain within the bounds of the array after the first move.
Return the maximum possible minimum value in gameScore after at most m moves.
 
Example 1:

Input: points = [2,4], m = 3
Output: 4
Explanation:
Initially, index i = -1 and gameScore = [0, 0].



Move
Index
gameScore




Increase i
0
[2, 0]


Increase i
1
[2, 4]


Decrease i
0
[4, 4]



The minimum value in gameScore is 4, and this is the maximum possible minimum among all configurations. Hence, 4 is the output.

Example 2:

Input: points = [1,2,3], m = 5
Output: 2
Explanation:
Initially, index i = -1 and gameScore = [0, 0, 0].



Move
Index
gameScore




Increase i
0
[1, 0, 0]


Increase i
1
[1, 2, 0]


Decrease i
0
[2, 2, 0]


Increase i
1
[2, 4, 0]


Increase i
2
[2, 4, 3]



The minimum value in gameScore is 2, and this is the maximum possible minimum among all configurations. Hence, 2 is the output.

 
Constraints:

2 <= n == points.length <= 5 * 10^4
1 <= points[i] <= 10^6
1 <= m <= 10^9

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxScore(self, points: List[int], m: int) -> int:
        
```

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

I recall a LeetCode problem "Maximum Score of a Good Array" maybe solution:
For binary search x, define need[i] = (x + points[i] - 1)//points[i].
Then:
ans = 0
for i in range(n):
    if i == n-1:
        if need[i] > 0:

For i < p, y_i can be 0 if previous y large enough. For i >= p, y_i at least 1.

Maybe there is an even simpler formula for MWIS with prefix increments. Since weights change monotonically by +1 on prefix. We can compute MWIS for all p using DP and maybe "slope trick".

Let's explore.

MWIS on path with weights w_i. Standard DP:
dp0_i = max independent set weight up to i not choosing i
dp1_i = max independent set weight up to i choosing i
dp0_i = max(dp0_{i-1}, dp1_{i-1})
dp1_i = dp0_{i-1} + w_i
MWIS = max(dp0_{n-1}, dp1_{n-1}).

As p increases, w_p? Actually at step p from p to p+1, vertex p-1 increases by1. Need recompute suffix? Since DP recurrence depends on previous, a point update affects all subsequent dp. Segment tree is natural.

But maybe because increments are prefix, we can compute MWIS(p) via two passes: For each p, the prefix vertices have +1. This is equivalent to choosing independent set with bonus for selected vertices in prefix. We can compute MWIS as max over independent sets of base weight + number of selected vertices in prefix. For each p, bonus = count of selected vertices with index < p. Need max over independent sets of base_weight(S) + |S ∩ [0,p-1]|. Let f(p) = max_S [base(S) + count_prefix(S)]. This is like parametric MWIS with vertex weights either b_i or b_i+1 depending on p. We need f(p) for p=0..n-1. Could compute using DP with "prefix count" maybe.

Another perspective: Since each prefix increment is +1, f(p) is the upper envelope of lines? For each independent set S, value = base(S) + |S∩prefix|. As p increases, count increments at selected vertices. f(p) is max of step functions. Need all p. Could compute via divide and conquer? But maybe O(n) possible with "Aliens trick"? Hmm.


Maybe minimal moves over p can be computed by the left-to-right greedy that at each index decides whether to "return" later. There is known solution with DP maintaining two states: whether we have already returned? Let's formulate walk directly.

A walk starting at 0, visits all vertices, can be decomposed into:
- Go right from 0 to n-1 (simple path).
- Along the way, for each vertex i, if need extra visits, do round trips on edge i (go to i+1 and back) before continuing right. This adds visits to i and i+1.
- After reaching n-1, if need extra visits at n-1, do round trips on edge n-2 (go to n-2 and back) or maybe after returning?
- Finally, return left some distance p to end. The return adds visits to vertices p..n-2.
But note some extra visits to vertices could be done during the return instead of round trips before reaching n-1. However doing a round trip on edge i before reaching n-1 costs 2 and adds one visit to i and i+1. Returning across edge i after reaching n-1 costs 1 (left crossing) and adds one visit to i (not i+1). Wait return traversal is cheaper per visit to i (1 move) but only visits left endpoint, and it also moves final position left. It cannot add visit to i+1. So if a vertex i+1 needs extra visits, round trips on edge i may be more efficient because they also help i+1. The return can help left vertices at one move each, but it forces ending further left, which may require more return moves. Actually returning from n-1 to p costs n-1-p moves and gives one extra visit to each vertex p..n-2. That's one visit per move, very efficient, but it also moves you left; if you don't need to end left, you could instead not return and do round trips? But returning might be necessary to satisfy left vertices? Wait left vertices <p get no return visits; they were only visited once on simple path. If a left vertex needs extra visits, you must do round trips on its left edge before reaching it? For vertex i<p, to get extra visits, you can do round trips on edge i (i to i+1) before moving on, which also visits i+1. Or you could go further right and return? But returning from n-1 to p stops at p, so doesn't visit vertices <p. To visit vertex i<p extra, you would need return all the way to i or do round trips. The return to p only helps vertices >=p. So optimal p likely chosen to let return cover a suffix of vertices, while prefix needs are handled by round trips.

This suggests a greedy: Process from left to right, maintain how many extra visits are still needed. The return from n-1 to p will cover one extra visit for each vertex in [p, n-2]. We can decide p based on where prefix deficits end.

z_{-1}=0.

- At end, adjust for last.

Define EMPTY = None. merge(L,R):
if L is None: return R
if R is None: return L
else combine.

In iterative segment tree with size power of two, leaves 0..n-1 are leaf states, leaves n..size-1 are None. Build:


Maybe we can compute M(p) for all p in O(n) using prefix/suffix DP and avoid segment tree. Let's revisit.

MWIS with prefix increments: We can compute M(p) for all p by two passes? Since weights are base + prefix bonus. Let's define for each vertex i, base b_i. We need M(k) = MWIS of weights b_i + [i<k]. This is a classic "maximum weight independent set with a bonus for prefix". We can compute for each possible k using DP with "bonus count" maybe via min-plus convolution? But bonus is +1, so M(k) is the upper envelope of functions base(S)+|S∩prefix|. The number of possible slopes is up to n. Could compute all M(k) using divide and conquer DP optimization? Let's see.

For a fixed k, MWIS can be computed by DP:
dp0_i = max(dp0_{i-1}, dp1_{i-1})
dp1_i = dp0_{i-1} + b_i + [i<k]
We need dp0_n,dp1_n. The only dependence on k is the indicator in dp1_i for i<k. For i>=k, no bonus. This suggests we can compute prefix DP up to k-1 with bonus, and suffix DP from k onward without bonus, then combine at k. Indeed, for a given k, vertices <k have bonus +1, vertices >=k no bonus. The MWIS can be computed by combining:
- Prefix DP over vertices 0..k-1 with weights b_i+1.
- Suffix DP over vertices k..n-1 with weights b_i.
Need combine at boundary between k-1 and k (adjacent). We can precompute prefix DP states for all k and suffix DP states for all k, then combine in O(1) per k! This is the key! Let's develop.

For each position i, we can compute DP states for prefix ending at i:
Let P_i[0] = MWIS of vertices 0..i with vertex i not selected.
P_i[1] = MWIS of vertices 0..i with vertex i selected.
Recurrence:
P_i[0] = max(P_{i-1}[0], P_{i-1}[1])
P_i[1] = P_{i-1}[0] + w_i
where w_i depends on whether i<k (bonus) for prefix part. For a fixed k, the prefix vertices 0..k-1 all have bonus. So we can precompute prefix DP using weights b_i+1 for all i. Let pref0[i], pref1[i] for vertices 0..i with bonus weights (b_i+1). This is independent of k for i<k.

Similarly, suffix vertices k..n-1 have no bonus. We can precompute suffix DP from right to left using weights b_i (no bonus). Need combine prefix [0..k-1] and suffix [k..n-1]. But the DP recurrence for suffix from k to n-1 depends on whether vertex k-1 (prefix last) is selected, because vertex k adjacent. We can compute suffix states for each starting k:
Let S_k[0] = MWIS of vertices k..n-1 with vertex k not selected.
S_k[1] = MWIS of vertices k..n-1 with vertex k selected.
We can compute from right to left:
S_i[0] = max(S_{i+1}[0], S_{i+1}[1])? Wait if vertex i not selected, then vertex i+1 can be either selected or not, so MWIS of suffix i.. = max(S_{i+1}[0], S_{i+1}[1]).
S_i[1] = b_i + S_{i+1}[0] (if vertex i selected, vertex i+1 cannot be selected).
This uses no bonus weights for suffix. Good.

Then for a given k (0..n-1), MWIS over all vertices with bonus on prefix [0,k-1] and no bonus on suffix [k,n-1] can be computed by combining prefix ending at k-1 and suffix starting at k:
If k=0, no prefix, M = max(S_0[0], S_0[1]).
If k=n-1? suffix starts at n-1, prefix 0..n-2 with bonus, combine.
For 0<k<n-1:
Let P = pref at k-1: P0 = MWIS prefix with vertex k-1 not selected, P1 = with vertex k-1 selected.
Let S = suffix at k: S0 = MWIS suffix with vertex k not selected, S1 = with vertex k selected.
Since k-1 and k adjacent, cannot both be selected. Total MWIS = max(
   P0 + max(S0, S1),  # vertex k-1 not selected, k can be anything
   P1 + S0             # vertex k-1 selected, k must not be selected
)
Wait if vertex k-1 not selected, suffix can choose k selected or not, so P0 + max(S0,S1). If vertex k-1 selected, suffix must have k not selected, so P1 + S0. This is standard.

For k=0: M = max(S0[0], S0[1]) where S0 is suffix all vertices no bonus. But our base w(0) has no prefix bonus, yes.
For k=n-1: suffix starts at n-1 (last vertex), prefix 0..n-2 with bonus. Combine P at n-2 and S at n-1:
S for last vertex: S0=0 (not selected), S1=b_last (selected).
M = max(P0 + max(0,b_last), P1 + 0).

This gives M(k) for all k in O(n)! Excellent. We don't need segment tree.

Weights:
For i=0..n-2: base b_i = a_i - 2. Bonus prefix weight = b_i + 1 = a_i -1.
For last: b_last = a_last -1. Bonus? Last never gets bonus, so prefix if k>n-1? k max n-1, prefix 0..n-2, last not included. Good.

Precompute prefix DP with weights pref_w[i] = b_i + 1 for i=0..n-2 = a_i -1. For last? We won't include last in prefix. We need pref arrays up to n-2. For n=2, prefix only vertex0.
pref0[0] = 0 (not select vertex0)
pref1[0] = pref_w[0] = a0-1
For i=1..n-2:
new0 = max(pref0[i-1], pref1[i-1])
new1 = pref0[i-1] + pref_w[i]
Store.

Precompute suffix DP with base weights b_i for all i=0..n-1:
For last i=n-1:
suf0[n-1] = 0
suf1[n-1] = b_last = a_last -1
For i=n-2 down to 0:
suf0[i] = max(suf0[i+1], suf1[i+1])
suf1[i] = b_i + suf0[i+1]
Here b_i = a_i -2 for i<=n-2. Note if b_i negative, suf1 may be negative; but max with suf0 handles. For suffix starting at k, S_k[0]=suf0[k], S_k[1]=suf1[k].

Then for k=0..n-1:
if k==0:
   M = max(suf0[0], suf1[0])
else:
   P0 = pref0[k-1], P1 = pref1[k-1]
   S0 = suf0[k], S1 = suf1[k]
   M = max(P0 + max(S0,S1), P1 + S0)
For k=n-1, S0=suf0[n-1]=0, S1=suf1[n-1]=b_last, works.

Great! This O(n) per check is much better. Let's formalize.

Given a_i = ceil(x/points[i]) for i=0..n-1.
If x=0 return True.

Let n = len(points).

Base weights:
b_i = a_i - 2 for i = 0..n-2
b_{n-1} = a_{n-1} - 1

Prefix bonus weights for vertices 0..n-2: pref_weight_i = b_i + 1 = a_i - 1. (For i=n-1 no bonus and not in prefix.)

Compute pref0, pref1 arrays length n-1 (indices 0..n-2):
If n-1 >=1:
pref0[0] = 0
pref1[0] = a[0] - 1
For i from 1 to n-2:
    new0 = max(pref0[i-1], pref1[i-1])
    new1 = pref0[i-1] + (a[i] - 1)
    pref0[i]=new0; pref1[i]=new1
If n==2, loop none.

Compute suf0, suf1 arrays length n (indices 0..n-1):
suf0[n-1] = 0
suf1[n-1] = a[n-1] - 1
For i from n-2 down to 0:
    b_i = a[i] - 2
    suf0[i] = max(suf0[i+1], suf1[i+1])
    suf1[i] = b_i + suf0[i+1]
Note: For i=n-2, suf0[n-1]=0, so suf1[n-2]=a[n-2]-2. Good.

Now evaluate k=p from 0 to n-1:
If k == 0:
    M = max(suf0[0], suf1[0])
Else:
    P0 = pref0[k-1]; P1 = pref1[k-1]
    S0 = suf0[k]; S1 = suf1[k]
    M = max(P0 + max(S0, S1), P1 + S0)
Then total_moves = 2*n - 1 - k + 2*M
If total_moves <= m: return True (early)
After loop return False.

Now, is the prefix/suffix combination formula exactly MWIS with bonus on prefix? Let's prove:
For a fixed k, vertices 0..k-1 have weights b_i+1; vertices k..n-1 have weights b_i. The path splits at edge between k-1 and k. Any independent set can be decomposed into independent set in prefix 0..k-1 and suffix k..n-1, with the only interaction that if k-1 selected then k not selected. The DP states P0/P1 and S0/S1 capture max weights with boundary selection status. Combining as max(P0+max(S0,S1), P1+S0) is correct. For k=0, no prefix, M=S0/S1. For k=n-1, suffix only last, formula works. Good.

In z formulation, z_i are extra round trips across edge i beyond the base path (simple path + return). But to perform an extra round trip across edge i, you need to be at one endpoint of edge i. The base path ends at p. If p < i, to do a round trip on edge i and still end at p, you must travel from p to i and back, which involves round trips on all intermediate edges. These are exactly additional z_j for j between p and i-1. Our greedy/MWIS should account for that through constraints? Let's check constraints for p=1, a=[2,3,1,2]:
c_i = a_i -1 = [1,2,0,1].
r_i = 1 if i>=p and i<=n-2: r1=1,r2=1, r0=0.
q_i = c_i - r_i: q0=1, q1=1, q2=-1, q_last=1.
z constraints:
vertex0: z0 >= q0=1.
vertex1: z0+z1 >= q1=1.

Let's write code carefully.

```
def can(x):
    if x == 0:
        return True
    a = [(x + p - 1)//p for p in points]
    n = len(a)
    # pref arrays for vertices 0..n-2 with bonus weights a_i-1
    pref0 = [0]*(n-1)
    pref1 = [0]*(n-1)
    pref0[0] = 0
    pref1[0] = a[0] - 1
    for i in range(1, n-1):
        p0 = pref0[i-1]; p1 = pref1[i-1]
        pref0[i] = p0 if p0 >= p1 else p1
        pref1[i] = p0 + (a[i] - 1)
    # suffix arrays for vertices 0..n-1 with base weights
    suf0 = [0]*n
    suf1 = [0]*n
    suf0[n-1] = 0
    suf1[n-1] = a[n-1] - 1
    for i in range(n-2, -1, -1):
        s0 = suf0[i+1]; s1 = suf1[i+1]
        suf0[i] = s0 if s0 >= s1 else s1
        suf1[i] = (a[i] - 2) + s0  # base b_i + suf0[i+1]
    # evaluate k
    base_const = 2*n - 1
    # k=0
    M = suf0[0] if suf0[0] >= suf1[0] else suf1[0]
    if base_const + 2*M <= m:
        return True
    for k in range(1, n):
        P0 = pref0[k-1]; P1 = pref1[k-1]
        S0 = suf0[k]; S1 = suf1[k]
        # M = max(P0 + max(S0,S1), P1 + S0)
        inner = S0 if S0 >= S1 else S1
        val1 = P0 + inner
        val2 = P1 + S0
        M = val1 if val1 >= val2 else val2
        moves = base_const - k + 2*M
        if moves <= m:
            return True
    return False
```

Now, let's revisit the dual/MWIS proof to be confident. We used z formulation:
Total moves = 2n - 1 - p + 2∑z_i.
Constraints for z:
For each vertex i, simple path gives one visit. Return from n-1 to p gives one extra visit to vertices i with p<=i<=n-2. Each roundtrip on edge i (z_i) gives one extra visit to i and i+1.
Need extra visits c_i = a_i -1.
Thus:
- i=0: z0 + r0 >= c0, r0=1 if p=0 else0.
- 1<=i<=n-2: z_{i-1}+z_i + r_i >= c_i, r_i=1 if p<=i else0.
- i=n-1: z_{n-2} >= c_last.
Let q_i = c_i - r_i (with r_last=0). Then z_{i-1}+z_i >= q_i, z_i>=0. Minimize ∑z_i. This is the dual of MWIS with vertex weights q_i? Let's verify dual exactly:
Primal min ∑_{i=0}^{n-2} z_i
s.t. z_{i-1}+z_i >= q_i for i=0..n-1, with z_{-1}=z_{n-1}=0, z_i>=0.
Dual max ∑ q_i y_i
s.t. y_i + y_{i+1} <= 1 for each edge i (i=0..n-2), y_i >=0.
Since constraint matrix is totally unimodular, optimal dual is MWIS on path with weights q_i (allowing negative weights, choose y_i=0/1). Thus min ∑z = MWIS(q). Good. q_i = c_i - r_i = (a_i-1) - [p<=i<=n-2] for i<n-1, q_last=a_last-1. This equals w_i(p) as defined. Good.

Now, prefix/suffix DP computes MWIS(q). q_i = b_i + [i<p]? Let's verify:
For i<n-1:
q_i = a_i -1 - [i>=p].
b_i = a_i -2.
If i<p: [i>=p]=0, q_i=a_i-1 = b_i+1.
If i>=p: q_i=a_i-2 = b_i.
So q_i = b_i + [i<p]. Last q_last=a_last-1=b_last. Perfect.

Thus M(k) = MWIS of weights b_i + [i<k]. Prefix/suffix DP correct.

Now, let's maybe derive the total moves formula from z one more time to ensure constant 2n-1 not 2n-?:
Start outside -1.
Simple path from -1 to n-1: moves = n (1 to0 + n-1 edges). Return from n-1 to p: moves = n-1-p. Extra roundtrips: 2∑z. Total = n + (n-1-p) + 2∑z = 2n -1 - p + 2∑z. For p=n-1, total = n + 0 + 2∑z.
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxScore(self, points: List[int], m: int) -> int:
        n = len(points)
        max_p = max(points)
        
        def can(x: int) -> bool:
            if x == 0:
                return True
            a = [(x + p - 1) // p for p in points]
            
            pref0 = [0] * (n - 1)
            pref1 = [0] * (n - 1)
            pref0[0] = 0
            pref1[0] = a[0] - 1
            for i in range(1, n - 1):
                p0 = pref0[i - 1]
                p1 = pref1[i - 1]
                pref0[i] = p0 if p0 >= p1 else p1
                pref1[i] = p0 + (a[i] - 1)
            
            suf0 = [0] * n
            suf1 = [0] * n
            suf0[n - 1] = 0
            suf1[n - 1] = a[n - 1] - 1
            for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
                s0 = suf0[i + 1]
                s1 = suf1[i + 1]
                suf0[i] = s0 if s0 >= s1 else s1
                suf1[i] = (a[i] - 2) + s0
            
            base = 2 * n - 1
            M = suf0[0] if suf0[0] >= suf1[0] else suf1[0]
            if base + 2 * M <= m:
                return True
            
            for k in range(1, n):
                P0 = pref0[k - 1]
                P1 = pref1[k - 1]
                S0 = suf0[k]
                S1 = suf1[k]
                inner = S0 if S0 >= S1 else S1
                v1 = P0 + inner
                v2 = P1 + S0
                M = v1 if v1 >= v2 else v2
                if base - k + 2 * M <= m:
                    return True
            return False
        
        lo, hi = 0, m * max_p
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi + 1) // 2
            if can(mid):
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid - 1
        return lo
```

Now, let's think about if the prefix/suffix DP can be done without storing `pref0` and `pref1` full arrays by computing prefix on the fly in k loop? We need pref[k-1] for increasing k; we could compute prefix incrementally in the k loop and combine with precomputed suffix. That would save pref arrays. Let's see:
We can precompute suffix arrays. Then in k loop, maintain P0,P1 for prefix up to k-1 as k increases. Start k=0 no prefix. For k=1, P0=0,P1=a0-1. For k=2, update with vertex1 bonus weight a1-1, etc. This avoids pref arrays. But we also need k=0 M from suffix. We can do:
- Compute suffix arrays.
- Evaluate k=0.
- Initialize P0=0, P1=a[0]-1 for prefix ending at0.
- For k=1..n-1:
   Use current P0,P1 (prefix 0..k-1) and suffix at k.
   After evaluating k, if k < n-1, update prefix to include vertex k (since next k+1 prefix should include vertex k). Wait loop k from1 to n-1:
At start k=1, prefix 0..0 = P. Evaluate M(1).
Then before next k=2, update P to include vertex1 (bonus weight a1-1) for prefix 0..1.
So we can avoid pref arrays. This reduces memory and maybe time. Let's implement this optimized version.

Detailed:
```
# compute suffix arrays suf0,suf1 as before.
base = 2*n -1
M = max(suf0[0],suf1[0])
if base + 2*M <= m: return True

# prefix state for prefix ending at 0 (vertices 0..0 with bonus)
P0 = 0
P1 = a[0] - 1
for k in range(1, n):
    S0 = suf0[k]; S1 = suf1[k]
    inner = S0 if S0 >= S1 else S1
    v1 = P0 + inner
    v2 = P1 + S0
    M = v1 if v1 >= v2 else v2
    if base - k + 2*M <= m: return True
    # update prefix to include vertex k for next iteration (if k <= n-2)
    if k < n - 1:
        new0 = P0 if P0 >= P1 else P1
        new1 = P0 + (a[k] - 1)
        P0, P1 = new0, new1
```
Let's verify with n=3:
After k=1 evaluate prefix0. Then update include vertex1. k=2 evaluate prefix0..1. Good.
For k=n-1, after evaluation no need update. This saves pref arrays. Need be careful: At k=1, P is prefix 0..0. At k=2, after update, P is prefix 0..1. Good.
For n=2, loop k=1 evaluate prefix0; no update. Good.

This is better. We can remove pref arrays entirely. Let's test with example2:

k=1 S at1 [0,98], inner98, v1=98, v2=0, M=98, moves=5-1+196=200. update include vertex1: new0=max(0,0)=0, new1=0+(100-1)=99 => P=[0,99].
k=2 S last [0,0], inner0, v1=0, v2=99, M=99, moves=5-2+198=201. min200. Good.

This optimized version uses only suffix arrays and a list. Let's adopt it.

Can we also avoid storing `a` by computing suffix needs a_i; we can compute a list. Fine.

Let's write can with this optimization:
```
def can(x):
    if x == 0: return True
    a = [...]
    suf0 = [0]*n
    suf1 = [0]*n
    suf0[-1]=0
    suf1[-1]=a[-1]-1
    for i in range(n-2,-1,-1):
        s0=suf0[i+1]; s1=suf1[i+1]
        suf0[i]=s0 if s0>=s1 else s1
        suf1[i]=(a[i]-2)+s0
    base=2*n-1
    M = suf0[0] if suf0[0]>=suf1[0] else suf1[0]
    if base + 2*M <= m: return True
    P0=0
    P1=a[0]-1
    for k in range(1,n):
        S0=suf0[k]; S1=suf1[k]
        inner = S0 if S0>=S1 else S1
        v1 = P0 + inner
        v2 = P1 + S0
        M = v1 if v1>=v2 else v2
        if base - k + 2*M <= m:
            return True
        if k < n-1:
            new0 = P0 if P0>=P1 else P1
            new1 = P0 + (a[k]-1)
            P0, P1 = new0, new1
    return False
```
Need ensure for k=1, P is prefix 0..0. For k=2, after update at end of k=1, P is prefix 0..1. Good.
For k=n-1, P is prefix 0..n-2 (after updates up to k=n-2). Let's trace n=4:
Initial P prefix0.
k=1 evaluate prefix0; update include vertex1 -> prefix0..1.
k=2 evaluate prefix0..1; update include vertex2 -> prefix0..2.
k=3 evaluate prefix0..2. Good.

This avoids pref arrays and is simpler.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxScore(self, points: List[int], m: int) -> int:
        n = len(points)
        max_p = max(points)

        def can(x: int) -> bool:
            if x == 0:
                return True

            need = [(x + p - 1) // p for p in points]

            # suf0[i], suf1[i]:
            # MWIS of vertices i..n-1 using base weights,
            # with vertex i not selected / selected.
            suf0 = [0] * n
            suf1 = [0] * n
            suf0[n - 1] = 0
            suf1[n - 1] = need[n - 1] - 1

            for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
                s0 = suf0[i + 1]
                s1 = suf1[i + 1]
                suf0[i] = s0 if s0 >= s1 else s1
                # base weight of vertex i is need[i] - 2
                suf1[i] = (need[i] - 2) + s0

            base_moves = 2 * n - 1

            # Final position p = 0: no prefix bonus.
            m0 = suf0[0] if suf0[0] >= suf1[0] else suf1[0]
            if base_moves + 2 * m0 <= m:
                return True

            # Prefix DP with bonus weights (need[i] - 1).
            # Initially prefix is just vertex 0.
            p0 = 0
            p1 = need[0] - 1

            for p in range(1, n):
                s0 = suf0[p]
                s1 = suf1[p]

                inner = s0 if s0 >= s1 else s1
                v1 = p0 + inner
                v2 = p1 + s0
                mw = v1 if v1 >= v2 else v2

                if base_moves - p + 2 * mw <= m:
                    return True

                # Extend prefix by vertex p for the next final position.
                if p < n - 1:
                    new0 = p0 if p0 >= p1 else p1
                    new1 = p0 + (need[p] - 1)
                    p0, p1 = new0, new1

            return False

        lo, hi = 0, (m * max_p) // n
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi + 1) // 2
            if can(mid):
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid - 1

        return lo
```
